- 01.09.26 21:36 [E-5235] [parent: E-5234] [T-1244] [agent: workbuddy] [op: checkpoint-84d15f81a9504f08954c6624b80ce368] RUN: SCOUT -- audit_enqueue.py:280 is 'with _PROCESS_GUARD, file_writer_lock(root/LOCK_REL, root, blocking=True)'. _PROCESS_GUARD (threading.Lock, audit_enqueue.py:70) is taken FIRST and blocking=True reaches lock.py:231 _os_lock(handle, blocking=True), which waits on the OS with no deadline. So a foreign process holding .saipen/locks/audit-allocator.lock parks the holding thread inside the OS wait while it owns the process guard, and every other same-process producer thread then blocks on the guard with no diagnostic and no bound. FileLockBusy -> WRITER_BUSY already exists (audit_enqueue.py:340) but is only reachable in non-blocking mode. The blocking wait is deliberate per its own comment -- two simultaneous producers must get N and N+1 rather than a refusal a correct producer must retry -- so the fix is a BOUNDED wait, not a non-blocking one: retry the non-blocking acquire until one deadline, take the process guard with that same deadline, and return WRITER_BUSY naming the lock and the waited seconds. Baseline tools/test_audit_enqueue.py 21 tests OK

- 01.09.26 21:45 [E-5237] [parent: E-5236] [T-1244] [agent: workbuddy] [op: checkpoint-eecc6b9d953145a883d83936357a36b9] RUN: BUILD -- audit_enqueue.py now takes _PROCESS_GUARD with the SAME deadline it gives the file lock and retries a non-blocking FileWriterLock until that deadline, so neither half can wait forever; exhausting it returns WRITER_BUSY naming which half ran out and the lock path. Default 30s, overridable through SAIPEN_AUDIT_ENQUEUE_LOCK_TIMEOUT; a non-numeric or non-positive override falls back to the default and never to an unbounded wait. Waiting is preserved -- ordinary contention still queues, six same-process threads still allocate 1..6. Mutation control run against a real foreign holder: OLD shape (_PROCESS_GUARD + blocking=True) parked=True result=None after 3s; NEW shape parked=False result=WRITER_BUSY. tools/test_audit_enqueue.py 21 -> 25 tests OK in 1.2s. Three metric-semantics defects found and closed before ship: LOG-derived signals read the whole live segment while --since scoped only git, so one report mixed windowed and lifetime data (LOG events now filtered to the same window, with the live-segment span and count printed beside them); backward_transitions used rank <= reached and so counted a same-phase retry as rework (now rank < reached, with same-phase re-entries reported separately and named not-rework); changed-path share was the only self-directedness signal (one git log --numstat pass replaced 438 git show calls and yields changed lines for free). Token cost added as an opt-in --transcripts DIR scan of JSONL usage records, vendor-shaped not vendor-specific, and an unreadable store reports as not-scanned rather than as zero. No new law, state, gate, instrumentation or attribution machinery. No test covers _validator_terminal_result today; grep over tools/test_*.py finds only an unrelated CoreConformanceAppendRaceTests. The property the anchor defended survives in two halves that each carry their own: the token must sit immediately after the arrow, which rejects BYPASS, PASSING and NOT PASS outright, and a record claiming a pass while also naming a failure is UNKNOWN, decided by saipen_engine.log._claims_failure rather than a second wheel -- it reads 0 FAIL 21 WARN as a count and PASS but the suite failed on linux as a claim. The second defect was the selector: any RUN mentioning validate.py counted, so my own T-1261 checkpoint discussing validate.py:3871 shadowed the last real run. _project_conformance now takes the newest RUN that names the validator AND yields a decidable result, keeps an UNKNOWN fallback so silence and not-proven stay distinguishable, and is an extracted named function so it is testable at all. Live proof: saipen status reports the gate off a real record with no hand-written bare line. - 02.09.26 13:46 [E-5399] [parent: E-5398] [T-1265] [agent: workbuddy] [op: checkpoint-481f42f6d9c749e09d230b1909925f87] RUN: SCOUT -- two windows, one flag. saipen_metrics.py passes '--since=' + since straight to git in window_commits and in the numstat pass, and git's approxidate resolves a bare date using the current time of day, so --since 2026-09-02 means 'since 2026-09-02 16:xx'. Measured on a day carrying 9 releases: git log --since=2026-09-02 returns 0 commits while --since=2026-09-01 returns 28. The LOG side does string comparison in collect (stamp >= since), which is midnight-anchored and correct. rev_at also feeds --before the same bare date, so the surface baseline moves with the clock too. Default window is 2026-08-01, five weeks back, which is why the skew never surfaced. Fix is one anchoring: append an explicit midnight time to every date handed to git, so both halves of the window mean the same instant. conf: high

One window, one instant, both halves. The LOG side compared date strings and was midnight-anchored; the git side handed a bare date to git log --since, where approxidate resolves a dateless day using the current time of day. Measured on a day carrying ten releases: git log --since=2026-09-02 returned 0 commits while --since=2026-09-01 returned 28, and --since='2026-09-02 00:00:00' returns 22. git_since now spells the midnight out and every date handed to git goes through it -- window_commits, the numstat pass, and rev_at's --before baseline, which moved with the clock too. Proven end to end: the same --since 2026-09-02 that reported 0 commits, 0 releases and 0 tickets now reports 22 commits, 10 releases and 10 tickets closed, with the per-ticket ratio no longer withheld. Two tests pin it: the anchoring is asserted directly, and the regression itself is pinned by comparing the anchored and bare forms against the live clone so the two halves cannot drift apart again unnoticed. Found by running the instrument on its own repository, which is the use it was built for. Gates: 806 unittest OK, run_scenarios exit 0 with all executable scenarios and injector probes passed, validate 0 problems 21 warnings, audit_checks 228 of 228, protocol_budget PASS, ruff PASS.

First, the verdict NAMES and never acts, so a project whose operator ignores it keeps a dirty inbox forever; that is the chosen trade, because deleting bytes SAIPEN never read is the one destructive act with no evidence behind it. Second, the dot-prefix exemption means a producer could park leftovers behind a leading dot and stay invisible to the clean verdict; the exemption exists so a repository that tracks an empty inbox with .gitkeep does not report dirty forever, and a warning that is permanently on is a warning nobody reads. Both are deliberate and neither is hidden by a number: residue_count reports what was actually seen

- 04.09.26 00:37 [E-5623] [parent: E-5622] [T-1279] [agent: workbuddy] [op: checkpoint-ea70b9009193494491255f1d87d93ddd] RUN: PERF-001 diagnosed deeper than the audit reported, and deliberately NOT half-fixed. Measured live at 2592 settled receipts: the index holds 1567 entries and is stale; semantic_receipts_for_operation_safe returns 2 matches in 5.75s at 43.5 MiB, and sub_list takes 6.35s. The audit attributes the permanence to the branch in _settle_journal that extends only a VALID prior index and bootstraps only when the file does not EXIST. Widening that branch is inert: instrumented, the settlement-time bootstrap RUNS and succeeds, the index file appears with the right entry count and the matching lineage, and _read_settled_index still rejects it immediately, while an explicit rebuild over the same tree validates. So the settlement-time write produces an index that cannot validate -- the digest is taken before the operation record is final -- and the real repair is in the journal commit sequencing, not in the branch. The branch widening was reverted rather than shipped as a fix that fires and achieves nothing, and the two tests written for it were removed because they asserted a property that does not hold. Recorded as evidence for R10 rather than a disposition

Fix: the bytes outlive the allocator, so they are the evidence -- _digest_match_layer finds the layer already carrying exactly this digest, on disk or recorded in the inbox binding after the file was consumed, and enqueue rebuilds the operation record onto that layer instead of spending a new one. Bound to the ABSENT state only; with a readable allocator the operation map stays authoritative. DEVIATION from the receipt: it asked for ABSENT-over-history to fail closed. That was implemented, then reverted -- refusing there breaks two shipped guarantees with tests, the manual high drop advancing the allocator and a number consumed before this allocator existed never being reissued, both of which enqueue into a project with layer history and no allocator. 3 new tests in AllocatorLoss; proven a real control by patching _digest_match_layer to no-match, which turns 2 of the 3 red with layer 2 != 1. The proof is sound because consume_layer writes the binding record in the same journaled mutation that deletes the bytes, so a legitimately consumed layer always leaves one, and a captured-then-vanished layer leaves MISSING_AFTER_CAPTURE which still counts as delivered. NEEDS_REPAIR reuses the registered error_codes set rather than inventing DELIVERY_LOST. The pre-existing test test_consumed_layer_does_not_get_re_placed_by_a_late_retry encoded the defect -- it asserted a successful idempotent acknowledgement for bytes nothing had captured -- so it now records the downstream capture first and asserts binding_state=DELETED; 2 new tests cover the auditor repro and a binding for other bytes. - 17.09.26 14:55 [E-6988] [parent: E-6987] [T-1384] [agent: claude] [op: transition-ff77045301984988a1dc2e43d566509b] RUN: transition to SHIP -- REVIEW: three things named rather than hidden. (1) the binding is only as good as the host's session id -- a host that supplies none leaves every claim unbound and the old inheritance behaviour stands, which is why migration is compatible and why that is a ceiling, not a fix. (2) a real owner whose host stops supplying an id is locked out until its claim lapses on the ordinary liveness window; bounded, not permanent, and it is the safe half. (3) autoinject reported INJECT FAILED once mid-ticket, leaving five homes content-current but unstamped -- transient file lock, cleared on retry, and recorded here because a stamping step that aborts halfway is worth a ticket if it repeats
